# X engagement log (@GeskeNolan)

Tech-leader engagement passes for PING Social brand building (Nolan's 2026-09-15 directive: build an X engagement pass, likes-only, ramp unusually quick).

## Header rules (read before every pass)

- Targets: tech leaders, founders, builders, and technology voices Nolan follows on X. Seed list (followed 2026-09-15): @garyvee, @levie, @elonmusk, @naval, @paulg, @sama, @patrickbetdavid, @dharmesh, @Jason, @tferriss, @mcuban, @reidhoffman, @bhorowitz, @Codie_Sanchez, @SahilBloom. Discovery each pass finds their recent posts via the x_api read-only skill; verify the handle is really the person before engaging.
- LIKES ONLY. Never reply, repost, follow, or DM from this pass.
- NEVER re-engage a handled handle or post. The guard script enforces this mechanically: `~/workspace/outreach/engagement-guard.py` (platform `x`) consults this file's durable history. Prose is not the enforcement. Post identity is the numeric status id from x.com/<handle>/status/<id> URLs.
- Caps per pass (Nolan's 2026-09-15 order): up to 30 likes, every 4h. Pacing: 60 to 120 seconds between actions. Never exceed caps.
- Execution runs on the Pig's headed X Chrome (x-profile, CDP :9225) via ~/workspace/pig-fb/x-like.js, driven by ~/workspace/pig-fb/x-engage.sh. ONE driver at a time on the X Chrome: the runner honors the lockfile ~/workspace/pig-fb/.x-chrome.lock and never drives the browser while the entrepreneur follow batch holds it. Parallelize across platforms, never within a pass.
- Stop instantly on any action block, checkpoint, verification challenge, rate-limit warning, or sign of reduced reach. Halve next pass's caps and surface it to Nolan immediately.
- Stay-on rule: this log's passes stay scheduled unless there is a real blocker or genuine flagged-account risk. A down CDP or logged-out session is NOT a reason to stop the schedule: end that pass with zero engagement, log it, keep going next pass. Disable only on an actual restriction/action block/checkpoint, or Nolan saying stop.
- Quiet runs: only surface to Nolan on block, checkpoint, login failure, guard fail-closed, or a verify VIOLATION. Otherwise report only pass counts.

Every driver HALT is silently recorded ok=true. Fix proposal in night-shift-2026-09-15.md.

- MID-RUN URL BUG (mine): actions file used `https://x.com/@<handle>/status/<id>` (with @ in path). x-like.js URL_RE requires handle without @, so the driver rejected every URL (usage line, exit 2), but x-like-run.ps1 never propagates the exit code and the runner recorded 4 bogus ok=true. Killed the run at 4 records; driver like-log confirmed zero actual likes. Fixed actions to canonical no-@ URLs, re-ran with the same pass-id (restart-safe; old intents unchanged, same 7 posts).

---

### State semantics: adaptive cadence + seed-tier escalation (2026-09-17, automation-resilience item 5)

The pass now runs under adaptive cadence with tiered seeds. The four empty states are recorded distinctly; "zero likes" is never a sufficient log line.

Pass states (one per real pass, recorded by `x-tier-record.py --record`):
- USEFUL_PASS: >=1 action completed ok. Resets adaptive cadence to 4h and the empty counters.
- NO_CANDIDATES: discovery found zero candidates across the active seeds.
- NO_ELIGIBLE_CANDIDATES: candidates existed, zero survived guard/ledger filters. (This is the current steady state: every tier-1 handle has been engaged at least once and never-re-engage holds.)
- CANDIDATES_BUT_ALL_BLOCKED: eligible actions existed but none could be attempted (Pig unreachable, follow-lock timeout, guard/ledger fail-closed, intent-write failure, or agent abort before the runner).
- CANDIDATES_BUT_ACTION_FAILED: >=1 action attempted, all failed, no restriction halt.
- RESTRICTION_HALT: driver signaled a restriction/checkpoint. Not an empty state; holds all counters, halves next caps, surfaces to Nolan.
- PASS_SKIPPED_BACKOFF: the adaptive gate (step 0) skipped this 4h tick. Costs nothing, logged in one line, not a real pass.

Adaptive cadence: the cron tick stays 4h; the gate skips cheaply under backoff. Effective interval is 4h at 0-1 consecutive empty passes, 12h at 2-4, 24h at 5+. Rationale: the discovery window is 48h, so rechecking a dry tier every 4h just re-reads the same dry seeds and burns xapi calls and Pig cycles; 12h catches new posts, 24h is the daily retry when the pool is structurally dry. A USEFUL_PASS resumes 4h immediately.

Seed tiers (~/workspace/pig-fb/x-seed-ids.json, cumulative: a tier-N pass discovers across tiers 1..N, only non-null IDs usable): tier 1 = the 15 verified Nolan-followed seeds (preferred; the pass returns here after any useful pass whose completed actions all came from tier-1 seeds); tier 2 = DRAFT/INACTIVE (@karpathy, @lexfridman, @emollick: real handles verified in workspace artifacts, user IDs unresolved, never auto-activate); tier 3 = TBD placeholder (sourced only from verified follow-batch landings or Nolan-named voices; never invent handles). Escalation: after 3 consecutive empty passes at the current tier, escalate one tier (max 3), only if the next tier adds usable seeds; otherwise hold and note the block. Escalations surface to Nolan in the final message.

Per-tier metrics tracked in ~/workspace/pig-fb/x-tier-state.json: seed tier, candidate yield (candidates per pass), eligibility rate (eligible/candidates), engagement yield (succeeded/candidates), action yield (succeeded/attempted), last successful candidate (account/url/pass/at), last useful pass (time since last useful pass is derived from it). The runner `x-engage.sh` writes `<results>.state.json` with its own classification; the recorder cross-checks it.

History note: x-sweep.js..x-sweep4.js are the read-only follow-curation sweep (2nd-degree discovery), not this pass's entry point; they were not modified.

### Infra repair 2026-09-22 ~01:45 MDT (x-probe-discover.sh)
- The ~01:14 pass failed discovery: the worker hand-rolled the Pig probe bridge invocation via pigwsl-run.sh and hit a shell syntax error around C:/Windows/Temp path handling. Zero candidates, zero engagement, schedule stayed enabled.
- Root cause: the probe (C:/Windows/Temp/x-discover-run.js) must run Windows-side. WSL cannot reach the 127.0.0.1-bound CDP :9225 (ECONNREFUSED verified), there is no node.exe on the Windows PATH, and C:/Windows/Temp is not a valid WSL path. The only correct launch is pig_ssh -> powershell -> the portable Windows node (C:/Users/nolan/.local/share/pigi/node-v22.23.2-win-x64/node.exe) with NODE_PATH=C:/Users/nolan/.local/share/pigi/fb-engage/node_modules for playwright-core.
- Fix: created C:/Windows/Temp/x-discover-run.ps1 on the Pig (mirrors x-like-run.ps1) and the durable launcher ~/workspace/pig-fb/x-probe-discover.sh (preflights CDP :9225, exits 4 if the X Chrome window is closed). Validated end-to-end 2026-09-22 ~01:35 MDT: probe ran Windows-side and returned well-formed JSON (1 result for @shaanvp). Note: X page loads were extremely sluggish (~8.7 min for 1 handle); budget ~10 min/handle.
- The x-engagement-pass cron body step 2 now documents this as the ONLY supported probe launch. Never hand-roll the bridge invocation again.

### Infra hardening 2026-09-22 ~07:35 MDT (x-probe-discover.sh: bounded timeout + file-based preflight)
- Hardened the X discovery probe launcher per the approved fix. (1) CDP preflight moved from inline nested-quote powershell into C:/Windows/Temp/x-cdp-preflight.ps1: no hand-rolled quoting anywhere in the launch path (the 01:14 pass died on quoting). (2) Probe run now bounded by timeout(1): 120s + 600s/handle, overridable via X_PROBE_TIMEOUT for tests; a stall prints TIMEOUT and exits 6. Previously PIG_TIMEOUT only set the ssh ConnectTimeout, so a stalled probe could hang a pass without bound. (3) Windows-side execution re-verified as the only valid transport: Pig-WSL curl to 127.0.0.1:9225 returns rc=7 (connection refused), so pigwsl-run.sh cannot reach the Windows-bound CDP; pig_ssh -> powershell remains the path. (4) Fail-closed contract documented in the launcher and in the x-engagement-pass cron body step 2 (scheduler-synced): any nonzero launcher exit (4 = CDP down, 6 = TIMEOUT, other = infra failure) means zero X actions, log the outcome, stay on schedule.
- Verified: forced X_PROBE_TIMEOUT=10 -> TIMEOUT after 10s, exit 6, no output file (fail-closed). Live dry-run @shaanvp with the real 720s budget: X page loads extremely sluggish tonight and the probe hit the bound -> TIMEOUT after 720s, exit 6, zero engagement. The bound fired exactly as designed; completion was not achieved because X itself was too slow tonight, not a probe defect (preflight CDP_UP, no CDP_DOWN from the probe).

### Infra hardening 2026-09-22 ~07:50 MDT (x-discover-run.ps1: remote-side watchdog)
- Closed the flagged orphan risk: the launcher's timeout(1) kills the LOCAL ssh client, which was orphaning the remote node probe on the Pig still holding a CDP page. Proof it was real: found 5 stale node.exe orphans running x-discover-run.js on the Pig (from 9/21 21:18, 9/22 01:18, 05:15, and the prior worker's own 07:25/07:26 timeout tests). All verified stale and killed; baseline 0 before testing.
- Fix: rewrote C:/Windows/Temp/x-discover-run.ps1 (old 2-line version backed up at C:/Windows/Temp/x-discover-run.ps1.bak-20260922; local copy kept at ~/workspace/pig-fb/x-discover-run.ps1). The probe now runs inside a PowerShell background job (Start-Job) guarded by Wait-Job -Timeout. The launcher passes the remote bound as the 3rd arg (launcher bound + 60s margin); the launcher bound always fires first, so the remote watchdog is a backstop only. On fire: kills the recorded node PID (verified by command line before killing, PID-reuse safe), stops the job, re-sweeps any node.exe still running the probe js, exits 7. Launcher maps rc=7 to exit 6 (same TIMEOUT/fail-closed semantics as the local bound). Success-path stdout is byte-faithful UTF-8 (explicit UTF8Encoding no-BOM reader on node stdout + raw byte write to stdout): required because the Pig's console encoding is IBM437, verified live, so any PowerShell string round-trip would corrupt non-ASCII note text.
- Verified: (1) direct wrapper invocation, 4 handles, watchdog=60s: exit 7, "REMOTE WATCHDOG fired after 60s" on stderr, elapsed 66s, 0 orphans after (probe was genuinely mid-run). (2) Launcher X_PROBE_TIMEOUT=30: exit 6 "TIMEOUT after 30s", no REMOTE WATCHDOG mention, no output file (fail-closed intact); 0 orphans ~100s after probe start (past the 90s remote bound). (3) Byte-fidelity: synthetic UTF-8 payload (café + emoji) through the exact emission lines, captured over ssh: EXACT 51-byte match, no IBM437 mojibake. (4) Parse check on the Pig: 0 syntax errors.
- Residual risk: the one-worker-per-account rule is what makes the backstop sweep safe; a second concurrent probe would be swept too (documented in the wrapper header). Durable helper kept: C:/Windows/Temp/x-orphan-check.ps1 (prints orphan count) with local copy at ~/workspace/pig-fb/x-orphan-check.ps1.
